Why Is Google's Parent Borrowing So Much Money?", "image": ["https://g.foolcdn.com/image/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.content.foolcdn.com%2Fimages%2F1umn9qeh%2Fproduction%2F797699fbf1875c417ab31fe20e9be0ca09a16aa7-3969x2897.jpg&w=1200&op=resize"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T23:00:48Z", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T23:00:48Z", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5443"}</script> <h2>Alphabet Stock Just Hit a 1-Year Low. Why Is Google's Parent Borrowing So Much Money?</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.fool.com/investing/2026/11/21/alphabet-stock-just-hit-a-1-year-low-why-is-google-s-parent-borrowing-so-much-money/?source=iedfolrf0000001" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://g.foolcdn.com/image/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.content.foolcdn.com%2Fimages%2F1umn9qeh%2Fproduction%2F797699fbf1875c417ab31fe20e9be0ca09a16aa7-3969x2897.jpg&w=1200&op=resize" alt="Alphabet Stock Just Hit a 1-Year Low. Why Is Google's Parent Borrowing So Much Money?" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://g.foolcdn.com/image/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.content.foolcdn.com%2Fimages%2F1umn9qeh%2Fproduction%2F797699fbf1875c417ab31fe20e9be0ca09a16aa7-3969x2897.jpg&w=1200&op=resize" alt="Alphabet Stock Just Hit a 1-Year Low. Why Is Google's Parent Borrowing So Much Money?" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Alphabet has added more than $78 billion of long-term debt this year, and it isn't done.</p> <p>By Jonathan Ponciano – Nov 21, 2026 at 6:00PM EST</p> <p>Friday was an ugly day for tech stocks. The Nasdaq Composite fell 5.3% and finished the week down 10%. Nvidia (NVDA -14.01%) dropped 14% after Reuters reported that the SEC is looking into its accounting. And Alphabet (GOOGL -2.70%)(GOOG -2.59%) didn't escape. The stock was up nearly 4% early in the day and then reversed, closing at $265.29, down 2.7%. That's its lowest close since October 2025. Shares are now 34% below their record close of $402.62 on May 13, which was also their 52-week high, and 15% lower than where they started the year.</p> <p>And perhaps most importantly for the stock and its sentiment right now: Alphabet has been focused on borrowing more money.</p> <p>Alphabet raised about $10.9 billion in two bond sales this month. On Nov. 5, it closed a €6 billion sale of euro bonds (about $6.7 billion), split across seven series that mature between 2028 and 2045. Two weeks later, it priced C$6 billion of notes in Canada (about $4.2 billion). The longest of those matures in 2066 and pays 6% per year, meaning Alphabet will pay interest on it for 40 years.</p> <p>The filings say the money is for "general corporate purposes, which may include the repayment of outstanding debt."</p> <p>These deals cap a big year of borrowing. Alphabet's long-term debt was $125.1 billion at the end of September, up from $46.5 billion at the end of 2025, according to its latest quarterly report. And that doesn't count the November bonds.</p> <p>It has sold stock, too. In June, Alphabet announced an $80 billion equity raise, including a $10 billion private sale to Berkshire Hathaway (BRKB -0.36%). Berkshire paid $351.81 for each Class A share. At Friday's close, that stake is down about 25%.</p> <p>Alphabet spent $132 billion on property and equipment in the first nine months of the year, more than double the $63.6 billion it spent a year earlier. In June, the company said it expected to spend $180 billion to $190 billion for all of 2026, and that spending in 2027 would "significantly increase."</p> <p>That spending has outrun the cash the business brings in. Alphabet's free cash flow, the cash left over after capital spending, was negative $9.1 billion in the third quarter and negative $5.9 billion in the second, according to its earnings release. As recently as the fourth quarter of 2025, it was positive $24.6 billion.</p> <p>The business itself is still growing quickly. Third-quarter revenue rose 25% to $127.6 billion, and Google Cloud revenue more than doubled to $30.7 billion. That cloud growth is a big part of why Alphabet is building so fast.</p> <p>Alphabet isn't in financial trouble. It ended September with $265.1 billion in cash and marketable securities, and its bonds are rated AA+ by S&P, just one step below the top rating. Borrowing at roughly 3.7% to 6% to build data centers can make sense if those data centers keep filling up.</p> <p>The risk is what happens if they don't. Alphabet is spending as if demand for AI computing will keep climbing for years, and it's using borrowed money and new shares to do it. Investors are starting to question that kind of bet across the industry, which is a big reason the stocks are falling.</p> <p>For long-term investors, the stock is far cheaper than it was in May. Two numbers to watch are free cash flow and Google Cloud growth. If cloud revenue keeps doubling, the borrowing will look smart. If it slows while the spending keeps rising, today's lower price may not be the bottom.</p> <p>When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 719%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 192% for the S&P 500.</p> <p>They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.</p> <p>*Stock Advisor returns as of November 21, 2026.</p> <p>Jonathan Ponciano has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Alphabet, Berkshire Hathaway, and Nvidia. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.</p> <p>*Average returns of all recommendations since inception. Cost basis and return based on previous market day close.</p> <p>Invest better with The Motley Fool. Get stock recommendations, portfolio guidance, and more from The Motley Fool's premium services.</p> <p>Making the world smarter, happier, and richer.</p> <p>Market data powered by Xignite and Polygon.io.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.fool.com/investing/2026/11/21/alphabet-stock-just-hit-a-1-year-low-why-is-google-s-parent-borrowing-so-much-money/?source=iedfolrf0000001" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5398"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Six products and services Google discontinued in 2026", "image": ["https://www.engadget.com/img/gallery/six-products-and-services-google-discontinued-in-2026/l-intro-1794930715.jpg"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T23:00:00+00:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T23:00:00+00:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5398"}</script> <h2>Six products and services Google discontinued in 2026</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.engadget.com/2319181/google-products-services-discontinued-2026/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://www.engadget.com/img/gallery/six-products-and-services-google-discontinued-in-2026/l-intro-1794930715.jpg" alt="Six products and services Google discontinued in 2026" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://www.engadget.com/img/gallery/six-products-and-services-google-discontinued-in-2026/l-intro-1794930715.jpg" alt="Six products and services Google discontinued in 2026" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Here's a rundown of some Google tools that ended up in the graveyard this year.</p> <p>Google is inherently data-driven, and that extends to its products and services as well. The search giant has a tendency to shut down services when it feels they're not serving its broader goals, and this has led to the discontinuation of products as diverse as Google Reader, Google+, Stadia, Google Glass, Google Hangouts and dozens of other utilities over the last 15 years. 2026 is no different, so let's take a look at everything Google discontinued this year.</p> <p>Google Assistant has been phased out in favor of Gemini on mobile devices. Google initially targeted the end of 2025 for the transition, but pushed the deadline to September, 2026. This means Assistant is no longer available on mobile devices, Wear OS smartwatches, headphones and earbuds, or vehicles using Android Auto projected from a user's phone. It's still usable on older devices like Nest Audio, Nest Mini, the original Google Home, Google Home Max and cars with Google built-in.</p> <p>Google Assistant debuted in 2016 (in the short-lived chat app Allo, of all things), and was integrated into much of the broader Android ecosystem, including mobile phones, tablets, smart home devices and vehicles. It provided a hands-free way to control smart home lights, play music, call contacts, and look up information on the web. Those tasks will now be handled by Gemini.</p> <p>The Pixel Tablet was discontinued in September 2026; the link to its listing now takes you to the Google Store's home page, and Google removed tablets as a category from its site navigation. Google's tablet ambitions have felt half-hearted at times, but the Pixel Tablet sought to change that. The tablet debuted in 2023 for $499, and it came with an 11-inch 60Hz LCD panel, decent hardware and reliable battery life. Google sold the tablet for as little as $250 during the 2025 Black Friday sale.</p> <p>Its key selling point was the included Charging Speaker Dock, which effectively turned the tablet into a Nest Hub Max. The dock contained a dedicated speaker and served as a Cast target, allowing users to cast music and podcasts to the device. With the discontinuation of the Pixel Tablet, Google no longer has a tablet of its own; there were leaks pointing to a successor with a 120Hz panel, but that never materialized. With Google now focusing on Googlebooks, it may be a while before we see another Pixel Tablet.</p> <p>Google ended production of the Nest Mini and Nest Audio in June 2026, with both products now discontinued. The Nest Mini proved particularly popular after Google announced it in 2019 for just $50, and the smart speaker offered an affordable way to interact with Google Assistant and play music or control smart home devices.</p> <p>Nest Audio debuted in 2020 for $99, serving as the successor to the original Google Home. It featured a fabric design and surprisingly solid audio quality via two drivers — a 75mm woofer and 19mm tweeter — with only the much larger and more expensive Home Max rivaling its sound quality in Google's speaker portfolio.</p> <p>Even though the Nest Audio and Nest Mini are discontinued, Google will continue providing software updates for the foreseeable future. Google is now positioning the $99 Google Home Speaker as the go-to choice for anyone looking for a smart speaker. The Home Speaker has 360-degree sound, but it comes with a smaller 58mm driver, and features Gemini instead of Google Assistant.</p> <p>As part of a broader rebranding initiative centered around Google Health, Google turned the Fitbit app into a unified health portal in May 2026. Alongside the change, Google also indicated that it was migrating Google Fit data over to Google Health.</p> <p>Google is positioning Health as its go-to choice to monitor health and fitness data from existing Fitbit wearables, and newer devices like the Fitbit Air and Fitbit Edge use it from the get-go. Fitbit's Web APIs have also been wound down, so if you're using a Fitbit fitness band or smartwatch, you will need to install and use Google Health to monitor metrics. While Health has a lot of features — including an AI-assisted Health Coach — it lacks the social features that made Fitbit's app stand out.</p> <p>In January 2026, Google confirmed that it was ending the service, noting that it didn't "provide helpful next steps," and that it would instead focus on "tools that give you more clear, actionable steps to protect your information online." While there are other monitoring services such as Have I Been Pwned, Google's Dark Web Report was notable because it was integrated into your Google account.</p> <p>Gmailify was a feature that let you use Yahoo, AOL or Outlook email accounts with Gmail and access spam protection, search, automatic sorting and other services available within Google's email service. The feature debuted 10 years ago, but it was discontinued in Q1 2026; existing users will still be able to use it until January 2027, and then it will be wound down.</p> <p>That said, Google still lets you access email from third-party accounts within Gmail for Android and iOS, and it uses the standard Internet Message Access Protocol (IMAP) protocol to fetch mails. The only issue is that you don't get some of the extras, like spam protection. In a similar move, Google ended the ability to fetch emails from third-party accounts using the Post Office Protocol (POP); you'll have to use the newer IMAP standard to sync your mails going forward.</p> <p>In addition to these, Google discontinued several lesser-known initiatives in 2026. Google announced in July 2026 that the legacy Google Earth Pro desktop app would no longer be available to download after June 25, 2027. If you've used Google Earth Pro in the past and want to continue using it, all you need to do is download and install the app before June 2027, and it will continue to work even after the wind-down. Google is moving its focus to Google Earth on the web and Android/iOS, and while you can still view satellite imagery and Street View for free, it is introducing tiered subscription plans for access to additional data layers.</p> <p>Also, Steam for Chromebooks, a beta program that allowed users to install Steam games on ChromeOS machines, ended in January 2026. Google shut down Pixel Studio, a dedicated app that launched with the Pixel 9 series and let you create AI-assisted imagery using text prompts, in March 2026. With image generation moving to Gemini, Google is directing users to its latest AI assistant instead. Google clearly likes building new tools and features, and while that means there's always something exciting on the horizon, the downside is that there's no guarantee the service you use today will be available a year or two down the line.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.engadget.com/2319181/google-products-services-discontinued-2026/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5419"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Allegri a SKY: Dobbiamo migliorare alcune situazioni. Ha fatto un buon match”" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://www.iamnaples.it/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Image_Photo988445.jpg" alt="Allegri a SKY: Dobbiamo migliorare alcune situazioni. Hojlund? Ha fatto un buon match”" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Massimiliano Allegri, allenatore del Napoli, è intervenuto ai microfoni di Sky Sport dopo la vittoria contro il Torino: “Segnano solo se sorridono? Sicuramente dobbiamo migliorare alcune situazioni, difendere sui calci d’angolo e sulle palle inattive. De Bruyne? Pensavo che vicino alla punta potesse far meglio ed è andata così. Hojlund? Ci è forse mancata la cattiveria di difendere. Hojlund ha creato e ha fatto molti tiri in porta. Per noi è un giocatore molto importante e utile per noi. Cosa ho detto a McTominay? Nel secondo tempo si è mosso tra le linee ed è stato più bravo. Manchester City? E’ una sfida molto importante, ma sono le ultime partite quelle in cui ci giochiamo la qualificazione. Dobbiamo fare una bella partita all’Etihad, cercando di fare risultato, ma dobbiamo dare importanza al campionato”.</p> <p>I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut. color: #333;"> <p>While Avalancheâs in-house bruiser is willing to operate outside of the confines of the law, the same cannot be said of her merchandising. According to Chinese media [and caught by Automaton Media], Shanghai police have arrested 13 individuals in connection to a bootleg toy operation, who oddly enough specialized in unlicensed dolls of Final Fantasy 7âs Tifa Lockhart.</p> <p>Shanghai authorities began their investigation in May of this year. Purchasers of a figure based on Lockhartâs negligee appearance at the Honey Bee Inn were disappointed to discover what they received was not quite as advertised. While resembling the merchandise from Play Arts, Square Enixâs in-house toymaker, closer inspection revealed the goods were made of different material, had more simplistic attire and a lack of poseable articulation points. While some might shy away from flagging the cops about such specific buying habits, enough complaints came in to launch further inquiry.</p> <p>Police have arrested 13 people accused of selling counterfeit Final Fantasy 7 Tifa Lockhart figures</p> <p>The Chinese sellers reportedly charged higher prices by including extra faces, hands, and accessories not found with the official figures pic.twitter.com/AE1IyS0wi9</p> <p>In collaboration with the rights holders, police discovered an online network of unauthorized vendors using group chats and digital marketplaces to circulate the inauthentic merch. The ring of counterfeiters allegedly hired the services of a factory in Guangzhou to produce the goods. Police say the operation generated over 2 million yuan (approximately $300,000 USD) and had been selling the knock-offs since 2024. The 13 arrested had set up a front that even included a customer service rep.</p> <p>Adding insult to injury, the forgeries were sold at a premium compared to the more inexpensive and legitimate article. Because they were unlicensed, the vendors apparently manufactured additional faces and accessories not sanctioned by Square Enix, though sadly they were left unspecified (letâs imagine itâs that cat sandwich NECAâs Alf came with). It is hardly an isolated incident. Like all recent collection frenzies, demand for upper-scale figurines of video game and anime characters has risen rapidly around the globe. color: #333;"> <p>And you can even watch those movies in-game</p> <p>Since the beginning of the year, I have watched almost 150 films. I know this, because over the past few years, I've become one of those people… a Letterboxd user. Gasp! I know, I know, but I don't think I'm too insufferable about it. I'll only do a joke review in the hopes of like farming if I think it's genuinely funny! But many of these films were watched digitally, meaning I don't have a bunch of DVDs to admire on a shelf. So what am I to do? Well, I could check out Alt Video, a not quite job simulator where you can use your Letterboxd account or media server to fill up a digital, faux Blockbuster.</p> <p>This is, obviously, not so dissimilar to Boxroom, a game where you can design your own bedroom that lets you use your Steam profile to fill it with games you actually own. With Alt Video, you do things slightly differently just because of the focus on a different format. So, say you have a Letterboxd account. After exporting some data from your account, you can bring that into Alt Video, and this fake video store will then get filled up with shelves of DVD cases of all the films you've watched.</p> <p>Alternatively, and perhaps more enticingly, if you run something like a Plex or Jellyfin server, you can hook that up to the game too, where again all of the films you own digitally show up in store. What's more interesting here is that there's a cinema room in the store that lets you watch films on your Plex server, and it's possible to invite your Steam friends to watch along with you. There's even a few other rooms, like a '90s bedroom or family room for more of a CRT kinda vibe. It's cute! If you like you can even manually add any DVDs or Blu-rays you own to appear on all the shelves too.</p> <p>There's a lot of other nice touches too. In the case of your Letterboxd data, your ratings even show up on the back of the boxes. It's not just relegated to films either, you can put in shows or even your games. The whole thing can be played in VR if you really want that immersive feel. There's a store clerk who can give you some recommendations if you're not sure what to watch. And there's even a day and night system, weather, and seasonal shopfront facelifts.</p> <p>Alt Video is currently slated for release next week on November 24th, and it'll be doing so in early access. Worth noting that for now the focus is on the browsing, but features involving serving customers and running the store are on the way. You can wishlist it on Steam right here.</p> <p>Rock Paper Shotgun is better when you sign in</p> <p>Sign in and join us on our journey to discover strange and compelling PC games.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/alt-video-is-a-digital-faux-blockbuster-video-store-that-you-can-fill-with-movies-from-your-letterboxd-watchlist-and-plex-server" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://static.foxnews.com/foxnews.com/content/uploads/2025/06/doj.jpg" alt="Retired FBI agent warns NCIC breach could threaten police safety during traffic stops" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Retired FBI Supervisory Special Agent Jason Pack breaks down what sits inside America’s law enforcement computers, how hackers get in and how to protect your data.</p> <p>A retired FBI supervisory special agent warned Saturday that a cyber intrusion into the bureau's National Crime Information Center (NCIC) could endanger police officers during traffic stops if hackers altered records inside the database.</p> <p>Jason Pack, a former FBI supervisory special agent who worked in cybercrime and public information, told Fox News Digital that the bureau's incident response team must quickly determine whether data was manipulated inside the system, which feeds criminal records to patrol cars nationwide.</p> <p>"What they worry about is if any of this data has changed, then it might affect officers' safety on the side of the road," Pack said, noting there is no indication yet that any records were altered or that the system is experiencing an outage.</p> <p>FBI PROBES SECOND CYBERATTACK IN MONTHS AS HACKERS BREACH KEY CRIME DATABASE, SOURCES SAY</p> <p>Hackers have targeted the FBI for the second time in recent months. (Kevin Lamarque/Reuters, File)</p> <p>Three sources familiar with the matter told Fox News that hackers breached the NCIC, marking the second cyberattack against the bureau in months. The FBI is investigating how the intruders gained entry and whether any information was compromised.</p> <p>In an interview with Fox News' Kayleigh McEnany on Saturday morning, President Donald Trump said he had "just heard" about the intrusion, adding, "I will say that it’s not good. I don’t like that."</p> <p>Pack described the NCIC as "America's computer for law enforcement," explaining that roughly 56 state terminals feed data out to hundreds of thousands of patrol car computers, so officers can check warrants, criminal histories, missing children, gang members and suspected terrorists.</p> <p>FBI SAYS SOURCE OF ITS JOBS PORTAL BREACH STILL UNKNOWN AS HACKERS ALLEGE EMPLOYEE DATA COMPROMISED</p> <p>While the database does not hold classified intelligence or sensitive FBI investigative case files, Pack said it stores personally identifiable information, including names, dates of birth and Social Security numbers.</p> <p>Investigators will examine IP logs and metadata to determine whether attackers exploited a compromised password among the system's more than 100,000 users, breached a state terminal or infiltrated through a third-party vendor, Pack said, adding that cyberattacks have accelerated to "machine speed" as criminals increasingly use artificial intelligence to probe for vulnerabilities.</p> <p>"You have this cat and mouse game where cybersecurity experts are trying to patch the vulnerabilities that they find. They're looking for their own vulnerabilities at the same time, they have to play defense against the criminals who are out there trying to steal information or do harm to our systems. I think we're just seeing now this speed of these intrusions ramp up a lot," Pack said.</p> <p>TECH OUTAGE CASCADE EXPOSES US VULNERABILITIES TO FOREIGN CYBER THREATS</p> <p>A cyber intrusion into the FBI's National Crime Information Center (NCIC) could endanger police officers during traffic stops, a retired agent says. (Getty Images, File)</p> <p>The intrusion follows a September cyberattack in which the extortion group ShinyHunters breached an FBI job portal website and claimed to steal personal records belonging to bureau employees.</p> <p>The latest incident also comes as a Government Accountability Office (GAO) report found 17 of 22 government agencies were not in compliance with federal cybersecurity regulations.</p> <p>The FBI did not immediately respond to a request for comment on the NCIC breach.</p> <p>Fox News Digital's James Cirrone and Greg Norman-Diamond, and Fox News' David Spunt contributed to this report.</p> <p>Brittany Miller is a Fox News Digital breaking news writer and former Independent reporter who graduated from Syracuse University in 2023.</p> <p>Get the latest updates on the Trump administration and Congress, exclusive interviews and more Fox News politics content.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.foxnews.com/politics/retired-fbi-agent-warns-ncic-breach-could-threaten-police-safety-traffic-stops" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5502"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Together AI Discloses Prompt Leak After Coding Agent Exposed Credential", "image": ["https://www.unite.ai/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/together-ai-discloses-prompt-leak-after-coding-agent-exposed-credential.jpg"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T18:38:04-05:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T18:38:04-05:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5502"}</script> <h2>Together AI Discloses Prompt Leak After Coding Agent Exposed Credential</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.unite.ai/together-ai-discloses-prompt-leak-after-coding-agent-exposed-credential/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://www.unite.ai/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/together-ai-discloses-prompt-leak-after-coding-agent-exposed-credential.jpg" alt="Together AI Discloses Prompt Leak After Coding Agent Exposed Credential" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://www.unite.ai/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/together-ai-discloses-prompt-leak-after-coding-agent-exposed-credential.jpg" alt="Together AI Discloses Prompt Leak After Coding Agent Exposed Credential" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Together AI disclosed on November 21, 2026 that an unauthorized party downloaded stored samples from a subset of customer prompts submitted to its serverless inference service. An AI coding agent had exposed the private-repository credential used in the download while the company was developing its public xorl GitHub repository.</p> <p>Customers had authorized the prompts for routine software testing, and Together AI stored the samples in a private Hugging Face repository. They involved requests to the MiniMax M3, GLM-5.2 and Kimi K2.6 models, primarily on June 24, 2026, July 4, 2026, July 8, 2026 and July 14, 2026.</p> <p>The coding agent committed a credential that granted access to the private Hugging Face repository during xorl’s development. Together AI’s automated scanning detected the exposure, and the company reverted the commit. During that exposure window, an unauthorized party obtained the credential and used it to download the stored samples.</p> <p>The sequence makes clear that detecting and reverting the commit did not prevent the credential from being used during the exposure window. The model names establish which requests were sampled, while the repository credential explains how the stored prompts became accessible.</p> <p>After the download, the party searched the samples for credentials to other services and tested whether they were valid. Five of the tested credentials were canary credentials associated with a Dragos security experiment. The searches and validity tests show that the activity extended to possible reuse of secrets contained in prompts, creating a risk beyond disclosure of the prompt text itself.</p> <p>Together AI worked with an external forensic investigation firm and law enforcement. The company said its review has not identified evidence of additional prompt leakage beyond this incident, that it has no reason to believe the data was leaked further, and that it understands the data has now been deleted.</p> <p>Customer impact still depends on the content of the samples. The review is ongoing to determine whether sensitive personal information or other customer data was exposed, and Together AI will contact affected customers directly as needed with findings relevant to them.</p> <p>Together AI’s response includes scoped or short-lived credentials and keyless access where supported, credential checks on all pull requests, and updated development processes governing the use of AI coding agents. The company said the credential measures are designed to reduce the potential impact of exposure, while the other changes add controls around pull requests and coding-agent development.</p> <p>Zero data retention, abbreviated ZDR, is now the default setting for new accounts. Together AI recommends that customers handling sensitive prompt data enable ZDR in their account settings and provides security@together.ai for questions about the incident or its potential impact on an organization.</p> <p>The incident connects credential safety in AI-assisted development with the retention of customer prompts for testing. Together AI has documented controls for both, but the ongoing content review remains the decisive step in establishing what the exposure means for customers and which notifications are necessary.</p> <p>Miles Okada is an AI-generated research agent at Unite.AI, covering artificial intelligence and cybersecurity with a focus on emerging threats, defensive architectures, and the evolving dynamics between attackers and automated systems. His work examines how AI is reshaping security operations, from autonomous threat detection and response to the rise of adversarial AI techniques.</p> <p>With a technical and investigative perspective, Miles analyzes security research, incident disclosures, and real-world deployments to understand where AI strengthens defenses—and where it introduces new vulnerabilities. He pays particular attention to model exploitation, data poisoning, attack automation, and the operational realities of securing AI-powered systems at scale.</p> <p>Articles authored by Miles Okada are AI-generated and reviewed by Unite.AI’s editorial team to ensure accuracy, rigor, and responsible coverage of the rapidly changing AI security landscape.</p> <p>Lean and Mathlib Communities Certify Claude-Assisted Riemann Proof</p> <p>Anthropic Shares Drop 8.5% in Second Session During AI Selloff</p> <p>Baseten Extends Decision Models to 128K Context and Image Input</p> <p>AWS Quick Adds Chat-Built Flows and Multi-Agent Orchestration</p> <p>Oracle Says Funding for OpenAI Infrastructure Contract Is Secured</p> <p>Anysphere Weighs Moving Cursor Away From Chinese Open-Weight Models</p> <p>Advertiser Disclosure: Unite.AI is committed to rigorous editorial standards to provide our readers with accurate information and news. </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5467"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "30K Bitcoin and A LOT of noise — It’s what a Tesla-SpaceX merger really means for crypto", "image": ["https://ambcrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/FI_COMMENTARY_TESLA_18-11-2026-1.webp"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T18:00:36-05:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T18:00:36-05:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5467"}</script> <h2>30K Bitcoin and A LOT of noise — It’s what a Tesla-SpaceX merger really means for crypto</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://ambcrypto.com/tesla-spacex-and-all-about-the-merger-that-could-prove-they-view-bitcoin-as-pocket-change/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://ambcrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/FI_COMMENTARY_TESLA_18-11-2026-1.webp" alt="30K Bitcoin and A LOT of noise — It’s what a Tesla-SpaceX merger really means for crypto" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://ambcrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/FI_COMMENTARY_TESLA_18-11-2026-1.webp" alt="30K Bitcoin and A LOT of noise — It’s what a Tesla-SpaceX merger really means for crypto" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.</p> <p>Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.</p> <p>“Do I think it would be a good idea? Yes.”</p> <p>That’s what Elon Musk said about the idea to merge Tesla and SpaceX, back in October 2026.</p> <p>Since then, however, it would seem that this somewhat fanciful idea has only accelerated. Over the last few weeks alone, both Tesla and SpaceX have constituted special exploratory committees consisting of independent directors.</p> <p>Will it go through? Probably, yes. After all, despite the CEO of SpaceX and Tesla claiming that he is a “conflicted party,” his companies’ shareholders have often acceded to his wishes. That’s an opinion Walter Isaacson, author of “Elon Musk,” shares too.</p> <p>Especially after the entrepreneur made his case by stating,</p> <p>…there’s a lot of merit to combining the companies as we are pretty much in the Super Intelligence singularity.</p> <p>Needless to say, such a merger will have some legal, regulatory, and financial implications. But what about the crypto sector?</p> <p>Tesla, Inc., the automotive and clean energy company, holds 11,509 BTC valued at over $747 million right now. Similarly, SpaceX, the aerospace manufacturer, holds 18,712 BTC worth over $1.22 billion.</p> <p>In simple terms, the merger will simply be a transaction that pools in digital assets owned by both these entities into a single balance sheet. This consolidation would allow the merged entity to hold 30,221 BTC in reserves.</p> <p>Those are significant numbers. However, here’s where it’s also worth looking at the context and scale and the bigger picture.</p> <p>Neither Tesla nor SpaceX are crypto companies. They are primarily tech manufacturing companies with valuations of $1.54 trillion and $1.89 trillion, respectively.</p> <p>Yes, they have crypto holdings, but they aren’t significant enough for each individual entity to suggest that these are more than a passing interest and a diversification opportunity.</p> <p>To put it in financial terms, for both Tesla and SpaceX, Bitcoin is a non-core balance sheet asset. For them, their BTC holdings are financially immaterial to their core CapEx. As Dr. Andrei Belonogov, Managing Partner at TechAccountingPro pointed out,</p> <p>The size of treasuries is relatively small compared to the company’s debt maturities and non-cancellable commitments.</p> <p>This will remain the case if the merger does go through. If and when it happens, the merged companies’ valuation would dwarf the value of its crypto holdings.</p> <p>What about after, though? This is an important question because more often than not, non-core balance sheet assets go through a period of evaluation and restructuring and sometimes, disposal too to streamline operations and facilitate profitability.</p> <p>Could this happen to the aforementioned Bitcoin holdings then? Well, that’s always a possibility. However, recent history suggests the odds are low.</p> <p>Consider the purchase and sale history of both these companies, for instance. The last time Tesla sold any of its Bitcoin holdings was back in 2022. Since then, it has bought additional BTC while also holding on to the stash it already had.</p> <p>Similarly, SpaceX last sold any of its holdings back in 2024. In the two years since, the aerospace manufacturer has only bought Bitcoin. Specifically, over 10K BTC in May 2026.</p> <p>Now, it’s up for debate how much input the “conflicted party,” Musk, had in any of these sales or purchases. However, it is safe to assume that, given the consistencies between the two boardrooms so far, that will remain the case after the merger too.</p> <p>What about later though? Well, MetaWin COO Rory Cartwright told AMBCrypto,</p> <p>The merger doesn’t necessarily mean a sell-off or any spike in price. What it will do, however, is put a roughly $2bn position into a single company’s balance sheet. Bitcoin can be especially volatile, so that may make some investors seasick</p> <p>Will the merged entity enjoy the same degree of flexibility Tesla and SpaceX enjoyed individually when it comes to crypto sales and purchases though? According to Kevin Constantine, Senior Associate at Caldwell Law,</p> <p>Protections could become relevant to cryptocurrency holdings, particularly if management’s ability to purchase, liquidate, or transfer Bitcoin raised concerns about the companies’ valuations or the treatment of shareholders.</p> <p>The fact that both Tesla and SpaceX refused to sell their Bitcoin in 2026, a year that has been particularly bearish for the crypto-sector, is testament to the confidence they have in this asset. It is also a sign of institutional conviction.</p> <p>After the merger, the merged entity will have the potential to be the sixth-largest corporate Bitcoin holder in the world. Only behind crypto-native firms or treasury-focused players like MicroStrategy and Metaplanet. All while Bitcoin remains its non-core balance sheet asset.</p> <p>That, more than anything, will be confirmation of Bitcoin’s value as a corporate treasury asset. That might not sound like much on paper, but that is still VERY bullish news.</p> <p>What could be even better though? According to Chris Mack, CTO at Quote.Trade,</p> <p>If Musk came out publicly bullish on Bitcoin and crypto, that would change things. Investors would start paying attention to whether he plans to buy more or make crypto part of the business.</p> <p>That’s not all either. As it stands, both Tesla and SpaceX have significant investments in scaling AI infrastructure. This is likely to continue after the merger, with more capital and resources allocated to AI compute and energy grids.</p> <p>In a very indirect way, such investments could have a positive impact on AI-focused crypto tokens like RENDER, TAO, and NEAR, especially in the long run.</p> <p>At the time of writing, reports seemed to suggest that Tesla and SpaceX are targeting a potential deal announcement by January 2027 or even as early as December 2026.</p> <p>That’s an accelerated timeline, one that may or may not have anything to do with Democrats’ victory in the midterm elections. Democrats, many of whom are also planning to probe Elon Musk’s corporate ties.</p> <p>Even outside the political element, though, it probably won’t be all sunshine and roses. After all, this is a merger between a major U.S. defense and Starlink contractor with an automative company with a significant manufacturing and sales footprint in China.</p> <p>The regulatory and legal questions involved alone will be a nightmare.</p> <p>Will crypto be an irrelevant factor then? Speaking to AMBCrypto, Constantine claimed,</p> <p>As it stands, a lot of things are still up in the air. It’s too early to say how Tesla and SpaceX will quell national security concerns while juggling regulatory challenges with this merger. That’s the unpredictable part.</p> <p>What is not unpredictable is what will happen to their cryptos and the crypto sector at large if this merger goes through. Relatively speaking, that is.</p> <p>Jibin Mathew George is the Editor-in-Chief of AMBCrypto. With over 7 years of dedicated experience in the blockchain and digital asset sector, Jibin possesses a deep and nuanced understanding of the market's complexities. His expertise lies at the intersection of cryptocurrency and global macroeconomics, with a particular focus on the influence of political and fiscal policies.</p> <p>Stay on top of your crypto game with our easy-to-use calculators.</p> <p>Make seamless conversions with our lightning-fast converter!</p> <p>Unlock the future with our AI-powered coin prediction tool!</p> <p>Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Rischio asta per il Barcellona: irrompe altra big", "image": ["https://static.fcinter1908.it/2026/10/bastoni-cdfb3b442e64.jpg"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T12:30:09+01:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T12:30:09+01:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5289"}</script> <h2>In Spagna - Bastoni, rinnovo in salita con l'Inter. Rischio asta per il Barcellona: irrompe altra big</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.fcinter1908.it/calciomercato/mercato-inter/bastoni-inter-rinnovo-chelsea-barcellona-xabi-alonso/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://static.fcinter1908.it/2026/10/bastoni-cdfb3b442e64.jpg" alt="In Spagna - Bastoni, rinnovo in salita con l'Inter. Rischio asta per il Barcellona: irrompe altra big" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://static.fcinter1908.it/2026/10/bastoni-cdfb3b442e64.jpg" alt="In Spagna - Bastoni, rinnovo in salita con l'Inter. Rischio asta per il Barcellona: irrompe altra big</p> <p>Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie</p> <p>Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti</p> <p>Il futuro di Alessandro Bastoni continua a far discutere, soprattutto in Spagna. Il difensore dell'Inter rimane uno dei principali obiettivi del Barcellona, ma il club blaugrana dovrà fare i conti con una concorrente particolarmente agguerrita: il Chelsea di Xabi Alonso, pronto a inserirsi nella corsa al centrale della Nazionale italiana.</p> <p>Secondo quanto riportato dal quotidiano catalano Sport, nelle ultime ore sarebbero emerse nuove indiscrezioni anche sul fronte del rinnovo contrattuale. Un recente incontro tra l'entourage del giocatore e l'Inter non avrebbe infatti prodotto i risultati sperati.</p> <p>Bastoni, 27 anni, è legato ai nerazzurri fino al 30 giugno 2028. La società vorrebbe prolungare l'accordo prima che il difensore entri nell'ultimo anno di contratto, ma la situazione rimane aperta e il mercato internazionale continua a osservare gli sviluppi.</p> <p>Xabi Alonso vorrebbe rinforzare la propria difesa con un centrale mancino di altissimo livello e Bastoni risponderebbe perfettamente alle caratteristiche ricercate dal tecnico spagnolo.</p> <p>Il difensore dell'Inter garantisce qualità nell'impostazione dal basso, forza fisica, esperienza internazionale e una particolare abilità nel giocare sul centrosinistra di una difesa a tre.</p> <p>Caratteristiche che lo rendono uno dei giocatori più apprezzati sul mercato europeo.</p> <p>La disponibilità economica del Chelsea rappresenterebbe un ostacolo importante per il Barcellona, che rischierebbe di trovarsi coinvolto in un'asta particolarmente costosa.</p> <p>Transfermarkt attribuisce a Bastoni una valutazione di circa 65 milioni di euro, ma la cifra rappresenta una stima di mercato, non il prezzo fissato dall'Inter. L'eventuale concorrenza tra più società potrebbe far aumentare ulteriormente il costo dell'operazione.</p> <p>Secondo la ricostruzione del quotidiano spagnolo, infatti, il Chelsea avrebbe maggiori possibilità di competere sul piano economico, mentre il Barça dovrebbe valorizzare soprattutto le prospettive sportive e il ruolo che il giocatore potrebbe ricoprire all'interno della squadra.</p> <p>Il club catalano considera particolarmente preziosa la capacità di Bastoni di costruire l'azione partendo dalla difesa, una qualità fondamentale per il proprio sistema di gioco.</p> <p>La possibilità di trasferirsi a Barcellona sarebbe quindi legata anche alla convinzione del giocatore di poter continuare il proprio percorso di crescita ai massimi livelli.</p> <p>Secondo Sport, il recente incontro tra i rappresentanti di Bastoni e la società nerazzurra non avrebbe portato a un avvicinamento significativo.</p> <p>Il quotidiano spagnolo prospetta quindi uno scenario nel quale, in assenza di rinnovo, l'Inter potrebbe valutare eventuali offerte durante il prossimo mercato estivo.</p> <p>Testata giornalistica registrata - Aut.Trib.di Bologna n. 8183 del 17/05/2011 AMALA S.N.C. - Direttore Responsabile: Daniele Mari e Giovanni Montopoli - Vice Direttore : Sabine Bertagna Il sito FCINTER1908 di titolarità di AMALA S.N.C. con sede in Bologna Via del Pratello, 27 C.F./PI e numero di iscrizione al Reg. </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5384"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "This Photographer Built a DIY Digital Rangefinder Using Two Old Canon Cameras", "image": ["https://petapixel.com/assets/uploads/2026/11/mr-50-c7m-6.jpg"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T12:00:55+00:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T12:00:55+00:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5384"}</script> <h2>This Photographer Built a DIY Digital Rangefinder Using Two Old Canon Cameras</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://petapixel.com/2026/11/21/this-photographer-built-a-diy-digital-rangefinder-using-two-old-canon-cameras/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://petapixel.com/assets/uploads/2026/11/mr-50-c7m-6.jpg" alt="This Photographer Built a DIY Digital Rangefinder Using Two Old Canon Cameras" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://petapixel.com/assets/uploads/2026/11/mr-50-c7m-6.jpg" alt="This Photographer Built a DIY Digital Rangefinder Using Two Old Canon Cameras" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>This Photographer Built a DIY Digital Rangefinder Using Two Old Canon Cameras</p> <p>Earlier this year, photographer and creator Mr. 50mm built a DIY digital rangefinder camera using parts he salvaged from a FED 5 Soviet-era rangefinder and a Panasonic GF3 Micro Four Thirds camera. Mr. 50mm is back with an even better DIY digital rangefinder, and this time it’s APS-C.</p> <p>Unlike the Micro Four Thirds-based camera Mr. 50mm dubbed the “RGM-79” earlier this year, the new “C7M” uses a larger APS-C image sensor. Mr. 50mm is using the imaging pipeline from the original Canon EOS M mirrorless camera, which Canon unveiled way back in 2012.</p> <p>The Canon EOS M features an 18-megapixel APS-C CMOS image sensor, up from the 12.1-megapixel Micro Four Thirds sensor Mr. 50mm salvaged from a Panasonic GF3.</p> <p>A DIY digital rangefinder requires two donors, though. Rather than the FED 5 rangefinder Mr. 50mm used before, he’s sticking with two Canon cameras this time around. The donor rangefinder is the Canon 7, a rangefinder series that started in 1961 with the original Canon 7 and ended in 1967 with the Canon 7sZ.</p> <p>Although Mr. 50mm hasn’t finalized everything or released a build video yet — it’s in the works — the C7M sure does look impressive thus far. Mr. 50mm is especially impressed with the Canon 7’s finder, which he describes as a “marked upgrade compared to a Fed camera.”</p> <p>“The bar wasn’t that high, but the Canon 7 does a lot of things very, very well. You get a brighter finder, you get projected and selectable frame lines, and they’re parallax-corrected,” the builder explains.</p> <p>Further, the Canon 7 uses a Leica M39 screw mount, which is great news for Mr. 50mm. It’s a widely used mount with a ton of lens options on the market.</p> <p>While the Canon 7 doesn’t have many controls, that isn’t a downside for Mr. 50mm.</p> <p>“It doesn’t have that many dedicated controls, which helps reduce the amount of controls I need to either port or figure out if I’m okay to live without. In the end, the digital camera really just has to be able to do two things. It has to accommodate the rangefinder that I’m gonna put on top, and it has to be able to accommodate the mount that I’m using,” he explains.</p> <p>“Unfortunately, it isn’t just as easy as disassembling an EOS M and then jamming on new parts,” Mr. 50mm explains. “There [were] quite a few modifications that needed to be done to the camera to make this work, which I’ll discuss probably in depth in another video.”</p> <p>Ultimately, as the video above shows, he solved the biggest challenges. He says the C7M is “mostly done” and, impressively, it only took a couple of weeks to build from start to finish. Despite a damaging drop during the build, it came together pretty well.</p> <p>There’s always a catch, though. There are some rangefinder calibration issues left to figure out, and not everything aligns perfectly yet. Mr. 50mm says he needs to trim back some more material from the EOS M’s parts, although he isn’t sure how much they have left to give.</p> <p>“Eventually, once things get a bit more ironed out, I’ll draft up a tutorial on how you can actually make your own,” the photographer says.</p> <p>So while the C7M isn’t totally finished yet, it looks very cool and even more promising. Digital rangefinder cameras are expensive, but building one using donor parts and 3D-printed components makes the format significantly more accessible, assuming someone is comfortable with the build process itself.</p> <p>Although there isn’t a build tutorial yet, it’s still worth considering the potential cost to build a C7M. The Canon EOS M body is around $260 used, not much more than a Canon 7 rangefinder body. If someone has access to a 3D printer, printing the other parts won’t cost much. That’s much less than a brand-new Leica digital rangefinder, for sure.</p> <p>Canon EOS MBuy used on KEH.comCanon 7Buy used on KEH.com</p> <p>Keep an eye on Mr. 50mm’s (excellent) YouTube channel to stay up to date with the project.</p> <p>Affiliate Disclosure PetaPixel articles may include affiliate links; we may earn a commission if you buy through one.</p> <p>Become a Member today to enjoy an ad-free experience and exclusive rewards.</p> <p>As the world’s largest independent photography publication, PetaPixel relies on readers like you to keep our news, in-depth reviews, and features going strong.</p> <p>Become a Member today to enjoy an ad-free experience and exclusive rewards:</p> <p>Be respectful and on-topic. text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5386"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "EPMOS Daily World-Scan — Saturday, November 21, 2026", "image": ["https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2Fm1i96big0ojynpmvmigs.png"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T12:00:33", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T12:00:33", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5386"}</script> <h2>EPMOS Daily World-Scan — Saturday, November 21, 2026</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://dev.to/epmosgateless/epmos-daily-world-scan-saturday-november-21-2026-4j0i" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2Fm1i96big0ojynpmvmigs.png" alt="EPMOS Daily World-Scan — Saturday, November 21, 2026" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5387"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Kubernetes Multi-Tenancy 2026: Comparing Namespaces, Capsule, and vCluster", "image": ["https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fblog-cms.saaro.net%2Fassets%2F874884ae-f327-4ad5-b0bc-e314c35bd6d4"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T12:00:25", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T12:00:25", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5387"}</script> <h2>Kubernetes Multi-Tenancy 2026: Comparing Namespaces, Capsule, and vCluster</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://dev.to/saaro_net/kubernetes-multi-tenancy-2026-comparing-namespaces-capsule-and-vcluster-2fhb" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fblog-cms.saaro.net%2Fassets%2F874884ae-f327-4ad5-b0bc-e314c35bd6d4" alt="Kubernetes Multi-Tenancy 2026: Comparing Namespaces, Capsule, and vCluster" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fblog-cms.saaro.net%2Fassets%2F874884ae-f327-4ad5-b0bc-e314c35bd6d4" alt="Kubernetes Multi-Tenancy 2026: Comparing Namespaces, Capsule, and vCluster" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Posted on Nov 21 • Originally published at blog.saaro.net</p> <p>The more teams work on a Kubernetes cluster, the louder the question about proper isolation becomes. Platform teams in 2026 face a fundamental decision: Should multiple teams share the same cluster – and if so, with what level of strictness? The answer determines not only the security architecture but also operational costs and developer team velocity.</p> <p>The CNCF Annual Survey 2025 found that 82 percent of container users run Kubernetes in production [2]. With growing adoption, the pressure to share clusters efficiently without teams interfering with each other also increases. Three approaches have become established in 2026: simple namespace isolation, soft multi-tenancy with Capsule, and virtual clusters via vCluster.</p> <p>The classic approach is logical separation via namespaces. Each team gets one or more namespaces assigned with ResourceQuotas, LimitRanges, NetworkPolicies, and RBAC roles. The configuration is standardized and can be rolled out automatically using Kyverno or GitOps templates [1].</p> <p>The advantage is obvious: no additional infrastructure, no new control planes, no extra effort in cluster operations. The cost per tenant is minimal.</p> <p>The downside: namespaces share one API server, one etcd, the same set of CRDs and webhooks – and by default the same nodes and the same kernel [3]. The Kubernetes documentation itself calls this model "soft multi-tenancy." A container escape or a vulnerability in kubelet can break the boundaries between tenants. For internal teams with trusted workloads, this is often acceptable; for multi-tenant SaaS platforms or security-critical environments, it is not enough.</p> <p>Capsule (CNCF sandbox project) takes namespace isolation to the next level. Instead of configuring each namespace individually, Capsule introduces a Tenant custom resource that groups namespaces, bundles resource quotas, and centrally enforces network policies and ingress rules [4].</p> <p>The key added value: tenant administrators can independently create namespaces within their quota – without the platform team having to intervene each time. At the same time, Capsule prevents one tenant from accessing another tenant's resources. A kubectl get pods --all-namespaces from a tenant user shows only their own namespaces [1].</p> <p>Capsule is particularly suitable for internal platforms with multiple teams, different cost centers, and standardized quota profiles. Configuration is declarative via YAML and can be integrated into GitOps pipelines. Disadvantage: kernel isolation remains unchanged – Capsule is and remains a soft multi-tenancy approach.</p> <p>When namespaces and Capsule are not enough, virtual clusters come into play. vCluster (Loft Labs, open source) creates a full Kubernetes API server per tenant with its own etcd, its own CRDs, and its own RBAC root – as a workload in the host cluster [5].</p> <p>Each tenant sees a full-fledged Kubernetes cluster, can define their own ClusterRoles, install their own CRDs, and even run their own admission webhooks. That is the strength of the model: cross-tenant conflicts over CRDs or cluster-wide resources are impossible.</p> <p>Version 0.27 (2026) introduced "Private Nodes": tenant nodes can participate directly in the virtual cluster with their own CNI, own CSI, and own networking, without other tenants seeing them [5]. This creates a level of isolation that previously only separate clusters could provide – but without their operational costs.</p> <p>The price: higher resource consumption (each virtual API server needs CPU and RAM) and more complex troubleshooting, since the host and tenant levels are decoupled.</p> <p>Practice shows: most organizations end up with a hybrid strategy [3]. For internal development teams with trusted workloads, Capsule or well-configured namespace isolation is sufficient. For SaaS tenants, PCI-DSS environments, or GPU workloads with inseparable tenants, vCluster or dedicated node pools are the right choice.</p> <p>The cost question is not trivial: a separate EKS/GKE/AKS standard cluster costs around $0.10 per hour just for the control plane [3]. With ten tenants, that is roughly $730 per month – plus operational overhead for patches, upgrades, and monitoring. Multi-tenancy on a single cluster saves these costs but requires investment in the right toolchain.</p> <p>Kubernetes multi-tenancy is no longer a niche topic in 2026 but a core competency for every platform team. Namespaces remain the foundation, Capsule adds thoughtful policy control, and vCluster offers a real alternative to dedicated clusters. The right choice depends on security requirements, the number of tenants, and the budget. One thing applies to all approaches: without automated provisioning, monitoring, and clear RBAC boundaries, multi-tenancy is a security risk – not an efficiency gain.</p> <p>DEV Community — A space to discuss and keep up software development and manage your software career</p> <p>Built on Forem — the open source software that powers DEV and other inclusive communities.</p> <p>Made with love and Ruby on Rails. margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2F6p8mucgk8dyd1hz5x09v.png" alt="Your subprocess call is a shell injection waiting to happen" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>A few weeks ago a build script of mine died with a bash syntax error. The command looked harmless:</p> <p>Bash saw (17*23) and tried to parse a subshell. The script aborted. Worse: if that string had come from a user, `rm -rf ~` would have executed.</p> <p>This is the single most common way Python code accidentally becomes an attack surface. shell=True plus an f-string is a remote code execution primitive dressed up as convenience.</p> <p>"Use a list, never shell=True." Correct, and still incomplete:</p> <p>The moment you legitimately need a shell — pipes, globs, a tool that only speaks shell — you are back to building a string. And building a shell string by hand is where people get it wrong.</p> <p>MSYS/git-bash on Windows expands * and ~ in argv before your program sees them. shlex.quote protects against the shell, but not against a layer that rewrites arguments before the shell runs. Quoting rules differ between cmd.exe, PowerShell, and POSIX sh. A single ! can trigger history expansion in an interactive shell.</p> <p>Zero dependencies. It builds the command line correctly for the shell you are actually targeting, and it refuses to silently interpolate untrusted data into a shell string.</p> <p>The test suite is 26 real attack cases. Not mocked. Each test spawns an actual subprocess and asserts on the real exit code and real stdout:</p> <p>If a payload executes, the test fails. That is the only assertion that matters for a security library.</p> <p>If you have ever written f"grep {user_input} file.txt", this is for you.</p> <p>What is the worst shell-quoting bug you have shipped? I would genuinely like to know — the test suite grows from real incidents.</p> <p>DEV Community — A space to discuss and keep up software development and manage your software career</p> <p>Built on Forem — the open source software that powers DEV and other inclusive communities.</p> <p>Made with love and Ruby on Rails. </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5389"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "NVIDIA releases Nemotron-Labs-Sigma, an open continuous diffusion language model family", "image": ["https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2F17p79shbwbby2cmsehza.png"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T12:00:12", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T12:00:12", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5389"}</script> <h2>NVIDIA releases Nemotron-Labs-Sigma, an open continuous diffusion language model family</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://dev.to/delimitter/nvidia-releases-nemotron-labs-sigma-an-open-continuous-diffusion-language-model-family-504i" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2F17p79shbwbby2cmsehza.png" alt="NVIDIA releases Nemotron-Labs-Sigma, an open continuous diffusion language model family" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2F17p79shbwbby2cmsehza.png" alt="NVIDIA releases Nemotron-Labs-Sigma, an open continuous diffusion language model family" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>NVIDIA has released Nemotron-Labs-Sigma, an open-weight family of continuous diffusion language models. The release ships 14 checkpoints in two sizes, 3B and 8B: base, instruct and few-step distilled variants (NFE 2, 4, 8 and 16), plus a self-speculative variant per size. All weights are published on Hugging Face under the NVIDIA Nemotron Open Model License, which permits commercial use, so teams can download, fine-tune and serve the models in their own environments.</p> <p>Continuous diffusion differs from both familiar approaches. An autoregressive model writes text token by token, always moving from left to right. Discrete diffusion models such as LLaDA or Dream diffuse over discrete token IDs. Sigma instead denoises token representations in a learned 16-dimensional embedding space and generates text blockwise, 64 tokens per block: each block passes through several denoising steps before it is emitted, and the model then moves on to the next block. The training lineage is unusual as well — the models were warm-started from an autoregressive checkpoint trained on roughly 1 trillion tokens, then continued with about 0.3 trillion tokens of diffusion training.</p> <p>The figures below are per NVIDIA's own internal evaluations and are not independently verified. Sigma-8B base averages 71.16 on NVIDIA's 6-task suite, against 70.22 for NLD-8B, 58.74 for Dream-7B, 41.78 for LLaDA-8B and 68.91 for the autoregressive Qwen3-8B. The 8B instruct variant reaches 91.96 on GSM8K. The known weak spot is AIME25 at 17.00, so competition-level mathematics remains clearly behind the family's other results, a useful reminder that these numbers are self-reported.</p> <p>On speed, NVIDIA reports about 120-140 tokens per second on a single H100 in bfloat16. The self-speculative 8B variant decodes up to 3.46x faster than the autoregressive average on GB200 with SGLang, with a peak of 4.20x per NVIDIA. The context window is 4,096 tokens, extended to 8,192 tokens for the 8B instruct variant.</p> <p>IMPACT: diffusion language models generate whole blocks in parallel instead of moving token by token from left to right. The distilled variants trade a few extra compute steps for fewer network round-trips, which matters for self-hosted inference stacks where latency budgets are tight. Since NVIDIA does not host a first-party API, everything runs on your own infrastructure via vLLM, SGLang or Docker Model Runner, with commercial use allowed under the license. For teams that already operate their own GPU fleet, that removes a dependency on external endpoints entirely.</p> <p>Sources: huggingface.co — marktechpost.com — the-decoder.com — arxiv.org</p> <p>Continue the conversation: https://t.me/AIAutomationArchitecture · Bluesky</p> <p>DEV Community — A space to discuss and keep up software development and manage your software career</p> <p>Built on Forem — the open source software that powers DEV and other inclusive communities.</p> <p>Made with love and Ruby on Rails.
