</script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5390"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "MariaDB support timeline 2026–2029: which LTS should your servers run?", "image": ["https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2Frmxu38op64kfrozuy88g.png"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T12:00:08", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T12:00:08", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5390"}</script> <h2>MariaDB support timeline 2026–2029: which LTS should your servers run?</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://dev.to/srvscripts/mariadb-support-timeline-2026-2029-which-lts-should-your-servers-run-572m" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2Frmxu38op64kfrozuy88g.png" alt="MariaDB support timeline 2026–2029: which LTS should your servers run?" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1200,height=627,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fdev-to-uploads.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com%2Fuploads%2Farticles%2Frmxu38op64kfrozuy88g.png" alt="MariaDB support timeline 2026–2029: which LTS should your servers run?" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Posted on Nov 21 • Originally published at srvscripts.com</p> <p>MariaDB changed its release model in 2024 and the consequences are now fully visible: instead of every version being supported for five years, there are short-lived rolling releases and, once a year, a long-term-support release that gets three years. For a hosting provider that means the “install and forget for half a decade” habit is gone, and the fleet needs a version policy rather than a version. This guide lays out the dates as they stand on 29 September 2026 and turns them into a policy you can apply.</p> <p>Short answer: As of late September 2026, MariaDB 10.6 is unsupported, 10.11 runs until February 2028, 11.8 until June 2028, and 11.4 and 12.3 until May and June 2029; 13.0 is a rolling release leading to a 13.3 LTS around mid-2027. Run an LTS with at least eighteen months of support left: 11.8 on cPanel until WHM adds 12.3, and 12.3 on DirectAdmin and standalone servers. Never put a .0, .1 or .2 rolling release on a customer-facing server, and keep the maintenance patch level current because that is where the security fixes are. (Reviewed October 2026.)</p> <p>In short: As of late September 2026, MariaDB 10.6 is unsupported, 10.11 runs until February 2028, 11.8 until June 2028, and 11.4 and 12.3 until May and June 2029; 13.0 is a rolling release leading to a 13.3 LTS around mid-2027.</p> <p>Each yearly series has four minor versions. The .0, .1 and .2 releases are rolling: supported roughly until the next one ships, meant for early adopters and testing. The .3 release is the LTS, supported for three years. So 12.0, 12.1 and 12.2 were stepping stones and 12.3 is what production runs. The same pattern is under way with 13.0, released on 15 September 2026, which will lead to a 13.3 LTS around mid-2027.</p> <p>Do not run a rolling release on a customer-facing server. They are fine for a staging box where you want to see what the next LTS will break.</p> <p>Note the oddity: 11.4 outlives 11.8 by nearly a year because the LTS windows were set at different points in the model change. If you want the longest runway from an 11.x install, 11.4 is it; if you want the newer features (VECTOR, VEC_DISTANCE_*, better JSON), 11.8 is the last 11.x LTS.</p> <p>The quarterly maintenance releases came out on 24 August 2026 across every supported series. Check each server against the current patch level:</p> <p>cPanel on AlmaLinux 8 and 9 offers MySQL 8.0 and 8.4 and MariaDB 10.5 through 11.8 in the WHM upgrade workflow; on AlmaLinux 10 the list narrows to MySQL 8.4 and MariaDB 10.11, 11.4 and 11.8; Ubuntu 24.04 gets MySQL 8.0 and 8.4 and MariaDB 10.6 through 11.8. 12.3 is not available in WHM yet. New cPanel installs default to 10.11.</p> <p>DirectAdmin’s CustomBuild tracks 10.6, 10.11, 11.4, 11.8 and 12.3, plus MySQL 8.0 and 8.4, and dropped MySQL 5.7 and MariaDB 10.3 in 1.693. It expects you to move through LTS versions in order rather than skipping.</p> <p>Standalone servers using the upstream repository can run anything on the list.</p> <p>Run an LTS that has at least eighteen months of support remaining. That single rule produces sensible decisions:</p> <p>New builds: 11.8 on cPanel until WHM adds 12.3, and 12.3 on DirectAdmin and standalone. Pin the choice in your provisioning scripts:</p> <p>Put the end-of-life dates into the same calendar or monitoring system that tracks certificate expiry and operating-system EOL. A simple cron that compares the running major version against a small table and emails when the remaining support drops below a threshold is enough:</p> <p>Feed that into whatever fleet inventory you keep; the MySQL health snapshot script already records the version per server so the comparison can be done centrally.</p> <p>Reading “12.3.3” as a minor point release that can wait. Within an LTS series the third number is the maintenance release, and those carry the security fixes. A server on 12.3.0 is missing every fix since May. Keep the patch level current through the normal package update cycle; on cPanel that is upcp and /scripts/update-packages, on DirectAdmin it is da build mariadb after da build update, and on standalone servers it is dnf update MariaDB-server followed by a restart in a maintenance window.</p> <p>Once a quarter, run the version query across the fleet, compare each result against the list above, and act on anything within eighteen months of its date. Each new LTS arrives around May or June; when one does, put it on the staging server within a month so that by the time you need it the what-breaks list for your own applications is already written.</p> <p>MariaDB support timeline: the questions this guide answers.</p> <p>Official documentation: MariaDB documentation, endoflife.date, Linux man pages.</p> <p>Related guides: MySQL 8.4 vs 9.7 LTS: what hosting admins need to know · Fix “Too many connections” on MariaDB/MySQL (cPanel) · caching_sha2_password in MariaDB 12: fixing MySQL 8 client compatibility · MariaDB latest version: live 12.3 LTS and 13.0 releases.</p> <p>Originally published on srvScripts — tested how-to guides and free tools for people who run servers.</p> <p>DEV Community — A space to discuss and keep up software development and manage your software career</p> <p>Built on Forem — the open source software that powers DEV and other inclusive communities.</p> <p>Made with love and Ruby on Rails. </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Join for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation plus sign you up to our newsletter.</p> <p>Get started with free access to reviews, badges and discussions.</p> <p>Unlock exclusive tools and insights for enthusiasts who want more.</p> <p>Access Bench, Roadmaps, deep analysis and other exclusive tools.</p> <p>Dive into our proprietary testing data and compare hardware with detailed benchmarks.</p> <p>Go beyond the headlines with expert reporting on the hardware industry.</p> <p>Track upcoming CPUs, GPUs and tech releases before they arrive.</p> <p>In-depth features, interviews and insider stories from the world of hardware.</p> <p>Expert insights and analysis delivered to your inbox.</p> <p>Dive into our proprietary testing data and compare hardware with detailed benchmarks.</p> <p>Go beyond the headlines with expert reporting on the hardware industry.</p> <p>Track upcoming CPUs, GPUs and tech releases before they arrive.</p> <p>In-depth features, interviews and insider stories from the world of hardware.</p> <p>Expert insights and analysis delivered to your inbox.</p> <p>Keep exploring and earning more as a member.</p> <p>Reviews, benchmarks, and updates on current GPUs.</p> <p>Join for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation plus sign you up to our newsletter.</p> <p>Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.</p> <p>Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.</p> <p>Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.</p> <p>Faster now, but the builder says the Optane sticks still need fans of their own.</p> <p>When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.</p> <p>A homelabber detailed improvements to the second-gen Optane AI build in a follow-up post on Reddit, showing the addition of two RTX 3090s to run the 2.8-trillion-parameter Moonshot Kimi K3 model at 3.11 tokens per second. Transferring almost half the model onto a RAM drive added a significant 41% to generation speed and 26% to prompt evaluation. The extra fans are still needed, though, as the persistent Optane memory gets very hot.</p> <p>That speed is not the most impressive, but it comes with 16.8 tokens per second for prompt evaluation, with 45 of 94 shards, or 414 GB out of 865 GB, living on a RAM drive. Such an arrangement is not new to enthusiasts who would set aside system memory for extra-fast performance in a pseudo-non-volatile manner — it’s still volatile, but appears like a disk — although not everyone has that much RAM sitting around free these days. With both GPUs but no RAM drive, the build ran at 2.20 tokens per second for generation and 13.3 for prompt evaluation.</p> <p>45 of 94 shards (414 GB of 865 GB) in a tmpfs RAM drive, the rest in Optane</p> <p>Builder’s measurements; llama.cpp, DAX on for the Optane portion.</p> <p>Even a quick read of the details makes it clear that the GPUs aren’t holding the model: two RTX 3090s, at 24 GB apiece, only have a total of 48 GB of VRAM, which is about 5.5% of the 865 GB model file. That said, the model is a mixture-of-experts (MoE) type, where its weights are split into multiple “experts” with only a few run per token. Any given token will have “routed” experts selected for it, while “shared” experts run on every token.</p> <p>For this model, with 896 experts, 16 are chosen per token, with two shared, with an active 104B parameters out of its 2.8T. All 2.8T parameters would take about 5.6 TB at 16-bit precision. Moonshot’s own release, which uses the 4-bit MXFP4 format, comes to about 1.56 TB, and the Q2_K file used here shrinks that to 865 GB, an average of under 3 bits per parameter. The 865 GB figure is likely in gibibytes (GiB), but for clarity we’re using the GB values as written by the homelabber.</p> <p>The configuration here has the routed-expert tensors stay on the CPU side, originally in Optane, and now in Optane plus the RAM drive. The RTX 3090s handle attention, or how each token weighs the rest of the context, and the dense feed-forward network layer that isn’t split into experts, where every token passes through it. They also manage the shared experts, other active-dense components, the KV cache, and the stored context of the current conversation, with the GPU compute buffers. Basically, the parts on the GPUs are “used on every token,” and the improvements there come from the high level of GPU compute and memory bandwidth.</p> <p>DRAM is a lot slower than VRAM, but it’s still capable of giving a decent speedup. The new build involves using Linux’s tmpfs, which, per the kernel documentation, “is a file system which keeps all of its files in virtual memory.” Those shards now live in memory, which admittedly is not ideal when the original build was designed to save money by using Optane instead. With those shards in DRAM, which the builder has said is faster than Optane, that part of the model no longer has to be read from the slower memory; the workload is decidedly distributed.</p> <p>Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.</p> <p>The speed that came with the GPUs is within the range of what the builder expected, guessing “somewhere between 2 and 4 tokens per second” in a prediction from the original build thread. The GPU install had to wait, in part, for modifications to the case, which required cutting out some of the metal and relocating the PSU to make room for the GPUs and their cooling. Cooling remains a huge issue, with “the improved throughput [not changing] the original thermal warning,” and the two 12W blower fans for the Optane DIMMs are kept in place. Add in dual 350W-class GPUs, and the changes are understandable.</p> <p>Things won’t stop here, as more optimizations are possible, including “customized quantization,” which may be similar to the techniques used by lawyer Nick Mermiges. Quantization selected per tensor or layer, or by where the data lives, might reduce bandwidth and capacity pressures for the slower tiers; the setup “might be faster yet.” Speed, or tokens per second alone, is not enough, and output quality must be taken into consideration in any future plan.</p> <p>Follow Tom’s Hardware on Google News, or add us as a preferred source, to get our latest news, analysis, & reviews in your feeds.</p> <p>Shane Downing is a Contributing Writer for Tom’s Hardware, covering consumer storage, PC hardware, and AI.</p> <p>Tom's Hardware is part of Future US Inc, an international media group and leading digital publisher. Visit our corporate site.</p> <p>© Future US, Inc. </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5385"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Harbinger is building the unsexy EVs that keep the economy moving", "image": ["https://platform.theverge.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/2/2026/11/gang-shot-7.jpg?quality=90&strip=all&crop=0%2C10.656236394691%2C100%2C78.687527210619&w=1200"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T12:00:00+00:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T12:00:00+00:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5385"}</script> <h2>Harbinger is building the unsexy EVs that keep the economy moving</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.theverge.com/transportation/1038448/harbinger-ev-startup-240m-series-d-fedex-army-trucks" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://platform.theverge.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/2/2026/11/gang-shot-7.jpg?quality=90&strip=all&crop=0%2C10.656236394691%2C100%2C78.687527210619&w=1200" alt="Harbinger is building the unsexy EVs that keep the economy moving" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://platform.theverge.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/2/2026/11/gang-shot-7.jpg?quality=90&strip=all&crop=0%2C10.656236394691%2C100%2C78.687527210619&w=1200" alt="Harbinger is building the unsexy EVs that keep the economy moving" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>While legacy automakers tap the brakes on electric vehicles, one startup is hitting the accelerator. Earlier this month, electric truck maker Harbinger raised $240 million in a Series D round, capping a banner stretch that includes a $300 million order from FedEx and a new defense contract with the US military.</p> <p>Harbinger is stacking up wins by skipping the consumer EV hype cycle altogether and focusing on the unglamorous workhorses that keep the economy running: Class 4–6 delivery vans and box trucks. The company’s thesis is simple: go after the overlooked commercial segments where legacy trucks are weak and giant automakers can’t be bothered to compete, CEO John Harris tells me.</p> <p>“We’re not solving the big obvious problem because everyone’s going to solve that problem, and as a result, the margins get kind of ground down to zero,” Harris says. “We’re solving problems that — they’ve just been there forever and you no longer think of this as a problem, you just think of it as like part of existence. It’s like a tax on your life.”</p> <p>Founded in 2021 by veterans of Canoo, QuantumScape, and Faraday Future, Harbinger aims to fill a structural gap in the auto market, in which most passenger EV platforms lack the durability to scale up while the heavy-duty platforms are too heavy and expensive to scale down.</p> <p>“In auto, you have Class 1 companies and you have Class 8 companies,” Harris says. “And everything in between is kind of a wasteland... It is much, much, much too small for a big company to serve.”</p> <p>Which is not to say the industry hasn’t tried. Ford and GM both took shots at electrifying the medium-duty segment with the E-Transit and BrightDrop delivery vans, respectively, but sluggish sales and the loss of federal incentives forced them to discontinue or cancel those models.</p> <p>Harris says that big automakers are ill-suited to task, with their over-reliance on third-party suppliers to build all the necessary components. The effort becomes too spread out and expensive, with not enough return on your investment. That’s why Harbinger is attempting a more vertically integrated approach, he says, assembling its own battery packs and drive units in house — with inverters to follow.</p> <p>“If you are not vertically integrated in auto, you don’t build viable products,” Harris says. “We believe that electric vehicles are only viable when they’re competitive on acquisition price with combustion vehicles.”</p> <p>Of course, Harris and his fellow cofounders have tasted failure in their previous ventures. Harbinger’s CTO Phillip Weicker cofounded EV startup Canoo, which ran out of cash and filed bankruptcy in 2025. Harris himself spent a year at tumultuous Chinese EV company Faraday Future before moving on to Xos Trucks and then Palmer Luckey’s Anduril.</p> <p>“I was an engineer at Faraday. I wasn’t in charge of anything,” Harris says. “So it’s not like, ‘John, why did Faraday not succeed?’ I can tell you lots of reasons, but it wasn’t because the plastic part that I designed didn’t work. It was because the plastic part that I designed was going into a vehicle for which the world customer base was like three people.”</p> <p>Those companies “fundamentally didn’t work,” he adds, “because of the market fit issue and the leadership issue. Not because the engineering didn’t work or electrification wasn’t ready or they didn’t have enough capital.”</p> <p>Harbinger’s approach is more pragmatic, offering both battery-electric and plug-in hybrid powertrains for its medium-duty trucks. The company is not pursuing heavy-duty Class 8 trucks like the Tesla Semi, however, as Harris argues the economics of electric long-haul trucking are unworkable.</p> <p>“Class 8 electrification to me is one of those markets. I completely reject the notion that that’s a good fit for electrification for the majority of the market,” he says. “We can put [the Tesla Semi] on the watch list with the Cybertruck.”</p> <p>With companies like Amazon and FedEx making serious moves to secure more electric delivery vehicles, Harbinger is well-positioned to be a key supplier. And with diesel prices skyrocketing as a result of the war with Iran, more companies are waking up to a reality in which EVs can help bring down their costs.</p> <p>Harbinger isn’t just building delivery trucks — it’s also branching into energy storage and military technology. For example, the company supplies battery power systems to Airstream and partners with American Rheinmetall and Forterra to build self-driving hybrid supply vehicles for the US Army. Harbinger also recently bought Phantom AI to bring advanced driver-assistance features to commercial fleets, in the hopes of giving companies a clear, measurable return on their safety investments.</p> <p>With millions in fresh capital and major contracts already on the books, Harbinger has cleared the first major hurdle that tripped up its founders’ past ventures. Now comes the hardest part in the auto business: proving it can actually build them at scale.</p> <p>“Auto is a big enough opportunity that it can get people’s attention,” Harris says. “It’s also punishingly difficult. So if you can make something that works in auto, it will be phenomenal in every other segment, because every other segment, the competition is essentially weaker.”</p> <p>Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.</p> <p>A free daily digest of the news that matters most.</p> <p>The Verge is a part of PMX Global, LLC, a subsidiary of Penske Media Corporation.</p> <p>Sign in to see your notifications or create an account to join the conversation.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.theverge.com/transportation/1038448/harbinger-ev-startup-240m-series-d-fedex-army-trucks" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5320"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "‘The Tree’ Director Julie Bertuccelli Tells IFFI Masterclass a Canadian Producer Stole Her ‘Italian Shoes’ Adaptation, Readies India-Set Feature", "image": ["https://variety.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Julie-Bertuccelli.jpg?w=1000&h=563&crop=1"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T11:55:26+00:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T11:55:26+00:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5320"}</script> <h2>‘The Tree’ Director Julie Bertuccelli Tells IFFI Masterclass a Canadian Producer Stole Her ‘Italian Shoes’ Adaptation, Readies India-Set Feature</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://variety.com/2026/film/festivals/julie-bertuccelli-italian-shoes-film-stolen-iffi-1236954904/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://variety.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Julie-Bertuccelli.jpg?w=1000&h=563&crop=1" alt="‘The Tree’ Director Julie Bertuccelli Tells IFFI Masterclass a Canadian Producer Stole Her ‘Italian Shoes’ Adaptation, Readies India-Set Feature" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://variety.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Julie-Bertuccelli.jpg?w=1000&h=563&crop=1" alt="‘The Tree’ Director Julie Bertuccelli Tells IFFI Masterclass a Canadian Producer Stole Her ‘Italian Shoes’ Adaptation, Readies India-Set Feature" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>French filmmaker Julie Bertuccelli, whose “The Tree” closed the Cannes Film Festival in 2010, said her planned adaptation of Henning Mankell’s novel “Italian Shoes” fell apart after a Canadian producer “stole the film” from her, and that she plans to shoot her next feature in India.</p> <p>Bertuccelli was speaking at “From Witness to Change: The Social Responsibility of Documentary Cinema,” a masterclass at the International Film Festival of India (IFFI) in Goa, moderated by Indian filmmaker Haobam Paban Kumar. Related Stories Documentary 'Porno Para Ricardo,' About Cuban Punk Musician Gorki, Shows 'the Struggle of a Free Spirit Trapped in a Cage' IDFA Head on How ‘You Can See Everything’ Phenomenon Can Pump New Blood Into Theatrical Doc Releases: ‘What Else Would This Audience See?’</p> <p>Responding to an audience question, Bertuccelli said she set out to adapt the Mankell book five years ago. She wrote the script and cast the film, which was due to shoot in Canada, before the pandemic brought everything to a halt.</p> <p>“In fact there is a bad Canadian producer who stole the film from me,” Bertuccelli said. She did not name the producer and declined to give the full account. She said she was forced to cancel the project, adding: “I worked for quite four years, five years for nothing because they stole from me, they stole, you know, the movie and I could not do it. So it was very hard.”</p> <p>Bertuccelli turned to the directors in the room. “It’s a fight against you, against yourself, because you need to work, you need even when you are depressed or you have doubts, you need to continue, because the only thing that makes you a director is that you go on and you never, you know, you never stop your desire,” she said.</p> <p>She is now writing the new feature. It follows a French woman living in Mumbai who is slowly going blind and sets off on one last journey with her son, who is half Indian and half French. Bertuccelli plans to come to India to make it.</p> <p>“It’s my first time in India, but for so long I’m admiring your country, your culture,” she said.</p> <p>Bertuccelli is also finishing a second documentary alongside “The Violin Lesson,” which follows Rachel Routier, a violin teacher in Pantin, outside Paris, and is headed for French cinemas after airing on France Télévisions in September. She hopes to screen “The Violin Lesson” in Mumbai in the coming weeks, along with either “The Tree” or “Since Otar Left.”</p> <p>During two terms as president of La Scam, the French authors’ society for documentary makers, Bertuccelli created the Golden Eye documentary prize at Cannes, whose Indian winners include Payal Kapadia’s “A Night of Knowing Nothing” and Shaunak Sen’s “All That Breathes.” She also founded La Cinémathèque du documentaire in Paris, which hopes to stage a retrospective of Indian documentary.</p> <p>Asked by Kumar whether documentary carries a social responsibility, Bertuccelli said: “For sure there is a big responsibility because the film can change the life of somebody, you know?”</p> <p>She pointed to “School of Babel,” which followed a class for children newly arrived in France. The film was widely seen by school groups and shifted young viewers’ attitudes toward immigrant classmates, she said, and it helped protect such classes at a time when some were being closed. “Because I said with this film to all the Education Ministry: ‘So I made a film to show that France is great, but don’t touch it. Now you can’t destroy this,'” she said.</p> <p>Kumar later asked where cinema stands in the age of AI. Bertuccelli allowed that the technology can be useful for small tasks, such as sound work.</p> <p>“But I hate AI. Not just because, okay, it’s dangerous for us, we will forget how to have memory, how to think by ourselves. Also because they steal, they stole the film, you know, they stole the author rights. But also more because it’s an ecological, horrible, horrible thing,” she said.</p> <p>Documentary, she argued, is cinema’s last line of defense, because “there is a contract with you that when you see a documentary, there is reality, there is truth.” She added that “the 21st century will be the one of documentary.”</p> <p>Bertuccelli spent around 15 years as an assistant to directors including Krzysztof Kieślowski, Bertrand Tavernier and Otar Iosseliani before making her own documentaries. Her fiction debut, “Since Otar Left,” shot in Georgia, won the Critics’ Week grand prize at Cannes and the César for best first film. Her documentary work includes “School of Babel,” “Latest News From the Cosmos” and “Jane Campion, the Cinema Woman.” “The Tree,” starring Charlotte Gainsbourg, played IFFI more than 15 years ago, but Bertuccelli could not attend as she was eight months pregnant at the time.</p> <p>She closed the session by pointing back to the Lumière brothers, whose operators traveled the world filming everyday life.</p> <p>“Cinema was born documentary and it will die documentary,” she said.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://variety.com/2026/film/festivals/julie-bertuccelli-italian-shoes-film-stolen-iffi-1236954904/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5371"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Investing - Theory, News & General • Re: Bad day after Really Bad Day", "image": [], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T11:52:50", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T11:52:50", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5371"}</script> <h2>Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: Bad day after Really Bad Day</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.bogleheads.org/forum/viewtopic.php?p=8902317#p8902317" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <div style="text-align:center; color: #333;"> <p>Join the club for quick access. Here’s how it works.</p> <p>Grand Theft Auto 6 is already enormously successful, having enjoyed "the biggest entertainment launch in history". But the current games industry is such that some Rockstar developers are still concerned about being laid off in the wake of its release. Writing for the Guardian prior to GTA 6's launch, Rockstar animation programmer Nestor Uriarte explained that although there is "no indication that layoffs are coming" it nonetheless "says a lot about the industry that even my colleagues and I, working on the biggest game of all time, are worried about getting laid off."</p> <p>It might sound odd for the developers of such a hugely successful game to be worried about losing their jobs, but layoffs don't just occur when games or companies struggle commercially. A common cause of layoffs in the games industry is companies trying to reduce overheads after a game has launched, as the studio switches from full production to post-release support for their current game and planning for the next one.</p> <p>It's partly why the industry seems to be in a constant, rolling state of layoffs, even when games appear to sell well. This issue was recently addressed by Jennifer Svedberg-Yen, the lead writer of Clair Obscur: Expedition 33. Svedberg-Yen, who left Sandfall Interactive in July, claimed that the problem is exacerbated by an imbalance in who benefits financially when a game like Grand Theft Auto 6 launches.</p> <p>"99 Metacritic score. Multiple award nominations before it even released. Massive sales and player engagement. Incredible amounts of tangible and intangible value creation. Yet the team is worried about layoffs," Svedberg-Yen wrote in an Instagram post. "So, who receives the lion's share of the profits from their labor? Not the vast majority of the creative team."</p> <p>Svedberg-Yen pointed out that this is "one of the issues that happens when the people creating the value do not receive a proportionate amount of that value," adding that "In finance, we call that value extraction."</p> <p>Svedberg-Yen also commented on the Rockstar team's ongoing efforts to unionise, with the team in the process of voting on whether or not it wants a union. If successful, the Rockstar Game Workers Union would become the biggest in the UK's gaming sector.</p> <p>"I sincerely hope the Rockstar team is successful in unionizing," Svedberg-Yen wrote. "Layoffs during lulls in development cycles would work a lot better if teams were properly paid for the value they create, not just one-time, but throughout the life of the thing they created."</p> <p>Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.</p> <p>The games industry has seen a huge number of layoffs in the last few years. A recent report by GDC found that 34% of developers it surveyed had been laid off since 2024. Xbox alone has cut thousands of jobs, with more planned going into January 2027. Most recently, Firaxis, owned by GTA 6 publisher Take Two Interactive, reportedly laid off a third of its staff earlier this week as it works on a new XCOM.</p> <p>2026 games: All the upcoming gamesBest PC games: Our all-time favoritesFree PC games: Freebie festBest FPS games: Finest gunplayBest RPGs: Grand adventuresBest co-op games: Better together</p> <p>Rick has been fascinated by PC gaming since he was seven years old, when he used to sneak into his dad's home office for covert sessions of Doom. </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5391"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "ChatGPT shrunk down to run on a Super Nintendo", "image": ["https://image.theregister.com/5311423.jpg?imageId=5311423&x=0&y=0&cropw=100&croph=100&panox=0&panoy=0&panow=100&panoh=100&width=1200&height=683"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T11:46:00.000Z", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T11:46:00.000Z", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5391"}</script> <h2>ChatGPT shrunk down to run on a Super Nintendo</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.theregister.com/offbeat/2026/11/21/chatgpt-shrunk-down-to-run-on-a-super-nintendo/5311418" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://image.theregister.com/5311423.jpg?imageId=5311423&x=0&y=0&cropw=100&croph=100&panox=0&panoy=0&panow=100&panoh=100&width=1200&height=683" alt="ChatGPT shrunk down to run on a Super Nintendo" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://image.theregister.com/5311423.jpg?imageId=5311423&x=0&y=0&cropw=100&croph=100&panox=0&panoy=0&panow=100&panoh=100&width=1200&height=683" alt="ChatGPT shrunk down to run on a Super Nintendo" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Just don't expect complex, coherent, or always correct answers</p> <p>If you’ve ever wondered how small an instance of ChatGPT could get, wonder no longer. Someone has actually rigged up a tiny version of the chatbot to run on a Super Nintendo cartridge.</p> <p>Tech journalist and retro tech enthusiast Benj Edwards is responsible for ChatGPT for SNES. He's published it online running in an emulator, complete with SNES-era music and sound effects, and for download as an SNES ROM, too.</p> <p>It’s not a big model, mind you, and performance is thus pretty limited: According to the website, it’s a 2.2 million-parameter offshoot of OpenAI’s GPT-OSS-120b, which was used to develop its training conversations – so in that sense it truly is a ChatGPT that runs on an SNES cartridge. Edwards described it to The Register as a distilled version of the aforementioned open-weight model.</p> <p>There are two options built into ChatGPT for SNES: One that’s purely the model, with every word generated coming right from its tiny little silicon brain, and another that includes a fact book culled from the opening sentences of around 150,000 simple English Wikipedia articles. If you opt to run both versions and ask it the same questions, you’ll notice a massive difference.</p> <p>Given some of the other responses we got while testing, there’s really no good reason to run the base model – memory isn’t really an issue given the basic build is about 4MB and the factbook build is just 19MB.</p> <p>Edwards admits that it’s not the most helpful AI model, either – this is just an experiment after all.</p> <p>“It is not a useful assistant: the model is millions of times smaller than the models behind ChatGPT, knows about 8,000 words, and often gets things wrong, sometimes confidently,” Edwards noted in his writeup. In that sense, it really is vintage AI on vintage hardware. “The fact book can carry mistakes too.”</p> <p>If you have an SNES lying around (lucky you), you can download the ROM from this link. If you want to get it running on an SNES you’ll need a cartridge that allows running of custom ROMs, and that supports Nintendo’s Super FX graphics coprocessor. Cartridges like the FXPak Pro and SD2SNES support it. The factbook version also requires support for the aftermarket Media Streaming Unit 1 (MSU-1), which adds CD-ROM like features to the SNES. MSU-1 is available in both of the flash carts mentioned above.</p> <p>If you have some real hardware skills, however, you could technically make your own ChatGPT cartridge.</p> <p>“The ‘pure’ version, which generates all responses from the AI language model, could run from a real custom SNES cart,” Edwards told us – it doesn’t even actually need the Super FX chip. “I do have a version of ChatGPT for SNES that can run without the Super FX chip, but without the extra processing power the answers are not as good.”</p> <p>That said, Edwards believes its absolutely crazy and nonsensical answers are part of the fun.</p> <p>“ChatGPT for SNES can definitely make mistakes too, but it's almost cute when it does it because of its limited knowledge and its playful interface,” Edwards told us.</p> <p>While a human may have designed the experiment, the coding work was done by AI, Edwards told us – he’s been interested in it since the 1990s and is pursuing more experiments with tiny AI models using the help of the bigger ones.</p> <p>“I've been theorizing about what I call ‘tiny talking machines’ … over the past year,” Edwards told us, and he’s been able to use coding agents to turn those ideas into actual software. ChatGPT for SNES is one example, and he also created an NES ROM called Séance that uses an even smaller AI model able to run on an original Nintendo Entertainment System that simulates talking to a ghost in a haunted game cartridge.</p> <p>“I designed the scope of what the software should be, how it should work broadly, what chip it should use, how the interface should look and work, and the sounds it makes when it types responses,” Edwards told us. He added that he sees the project as an AI/human collaboration, which is where he believes AI works best.</p> <p>“The AI agents who did the coding for this project currently advise that we've hit the limit of how good the AI model can be on the limited hardware,” Edwards said - he’s not taking their word for it, though. “It's up to human creativity to transcend those assumptions. I have already come up with new ideas that I believe can make a tiny model like ChatGPT for SNES even more coherent, and I'm working on them now.” ®</p> <p>Just don't expect complex, coherent, or always correct answers</p> <p>It isn't only systems on Earth suffering technical problems</p> <p>PARTNER CONTENT: As carriers tighten underwriting, a backup job marked green is no longer enough. Here’s how it works.</p> <p>Stealing cars in GTA 6 is one of the easiest ways to attract attention from the police, thanks to the new Wanted system. You've probably noticed by now that breaking into vehicles is noisy and messy, with Jason and Lucia needing to smash the window to get inside. It also takes time – precious time in which some nosey onlooker can report your description and activity to the cops. There has to be an easier way, right? There is. Which is why you need to learn how to get a Slim Jim in GTA 6.</p> <p>The Slim Jim in GTA 6 is only available from Chapter 2 onwards. This essential carjacking tool will unlock as part of the main GTA 6 mission list, coming into your possession after you begin a quest called 'Moses'. It's in this mission where you'll meet your first vehicle fence, learn how to sell cars and bikes in GTA 6, and be given the information you need to better survey vehicles for security features with the WAiNK app – which can be activated at any time by holding Up on the D-pad.</p> <p>Moses will give you the Slim Jim as a part of this mission. It'll let you get into any vehicle without needing to smash the window. You will, however, still need to contend with security features – you'll have to wait until Chapter 3 to learn how to get the Key Cloner in GTA 6 and understand how to deactivate trackers in GTA 6 to steal more modern vehicles.</p> <p>Once you've got it, using the Slim Jim is GTA 6 is easy enough. Walk up to any vehicle you want to try and steal. Walk up to the driver-side or passenger door, and press Triangle/Y to bring out your Slim Jim. From there, it's a case of rotating the right thumbstick in a curve until you find the sweet spot – which you'll spot by rumble in the controller and by a slightly pink hue to the semi-circle indicator. Eventually you'll hear a little pop, meaning you can now climb inside.</p> <p>Find all of our GTA 6 Guides or scroll through below to find everything you need to know about vehicles in the game.</p> <p>Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more</p> <p>Josh West is Editor-in-Chief of GamesRadar+. Now 3 of America’s 4 largest districts are pushing back", "image": ["https://fortune.com/img-assets/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/GettyImages-1254051142.jpg?resize=1200,600"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T11:40:00", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T11:40:00", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5342"}</script> <h2>Google told schools to accept AI or lose Search. Now 3 of America’s 4 largest districts are pushing back</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://fortune.com/2026/11/21/google-search-ai-overviews-schools-chicago-nyc-los-angeles-miami/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://fortune.com/img-assets/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/GettyImages-1254051142.jpg?resize=1200,600" alt="Google told schools to accept AI or lose Search. Now 3 of America’s 4 largest districts are pushing back" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://fortune.com/img-assets/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/GettyImages-1254051142.jpg?resize=1200,600" alt="Google told schools to accept AI or lose Search. Now 3 of America’s 4 largest districts are pushing back" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Google has handed school districts what many have called an ultimatum: Allow students to see AI summaries in their Google searches, or give up Google Search on school accounts altogether.</p> <p>In August, Google switched on a Gemini tab in Google Classroom by default for K-12 students whose schools had already allowed its AI chatbot, Gemini. On Nov. 3, it reversed course, announcing that its AI tools will be off for K-12 student accounts unless school administrators turn them on, starting early next year. But Google also made clear that schools that want students to have Google Search will have to accept AI Overviews, the AI-generated summaries that have become a familiar sight at the top of search results.</p> <p>“Search and AI are deeply integrated,” Chris Phillips, vice president for education at Google, told The New York Times, adding that students at schools that don’t allow AI “will not have access to search, and they’ll see a message from their school that indicates it’s not available.”</p> <p>Many school districts have restricted or banned student AI use, citing safety and privacy concerns. They may not have expected those restrictions to cost them the world’s most popular search engine, a trade-off that has left edtech experts questioning Google’s rationale.</p> <p>Google’s decision comes as tech companies face mounting legal pressure over harm to children. In March, a Los Angeles jury found Meta and Google’s YouTube negligent and determined they failed to warn users about the dangers of their platforms. In January, Google and Character.AI, a chatbot company with ties to Google, agreed to settle lawsuits from families who said their children were harmed by its chatbots.</p> <p>And earlier this month, a family sued Google, alleging their 13-year-old daughter used Gemini on her school-issued Chromebook to plan her suicide and that Google pushed its AI products on students without warning parents or schools.</p> <p>“We hear parents and teachers’ concerns about students using AI to shortcut learning, and we share the same goal: help students learn,” a Google spokesperson said, adding that the company is piloting new Gemini features to support “productive struggle” by helping students “work through problems without skipping straight to answers.”</p> <p>Whether that’s enough to win over districts that have restricted AI is another question. The country’s four largest school districts have landed in different places, both on AI and on Google’s decision to tie it to Search.</p> <p>Alphabet, Google’s parent company, did not respond to Fortune‘s request for comment.</p> <p>Chicago Public Schools has pushed back the hardest. On Dec. 22, 2025, the district began blocking students from AI tools such as ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and other third-party AI products. Students may use only AI-enhanced edtech tools vetted through the district’s compliance process under SOPPA, Illinois’ student data privacy law.</p> <p>On Nov. 11, about 30 protesters gathered outside Google’s West Loop office, chanting, “Hey Google, nice try. We don’t want your Gemini.”</p> <p>In a statement to the Chicago Sun-Times, a CPS spokesperson said the district is “actively exploring all options to independently suppress” the AI Overviews in Google searches.</p> <p>“The District would like to continue to provide students with access to the Google search engine without the AI component. If such an arrangement can’t be reached, the District will look to other search solutions,” the spokesperson said.</p> <p>New York City Public Schools, the nation’s largest district, appears headed in a similar direction, though it has yet to decide. On Sept. 2, Mayor Zohran Mamdani and Schools Chancellor Kamar Samuels announced a one-year moratorium on student-facing AI for 2-K through eighth grade. They also banned companion chatbots in all grades.</p> <p>After Google’s announcement, Mamdani posted on X that “Google is trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools. No corporation should have the power to dictate how we teach our children.”</p> <p>City Council Speaker Julie Menin and Council Member Shekar Krishnan sent a letter to Google CEO Sundar Pichai asking whether Google would let students keep using Google Search without AI Overviews and, if not, why not, given Google’s record of restricting product features for young users, as it does with YouTube Kids. The stakes are large: The district has about 1.3 million Chromebooks, including 500,000 assigned to students, according to the letter.</p> <p>District spokesperson Nicole Brownstein told Gothamist that officials are “reaching out to Google to discuss this update further,” and that the district had already disabled the Gemini chatbot and AI Mode on student Chromebooks. If Google doesn’t budge, the nation’s largest school district will have to choose between softening its stance and finding another search engine.</p> <p>Los Angeles Unified, the nation’s second-largest district, announced on Sept. 2 that it would block generative AI for students in all grades on district-issued devices for the 2026-27 school year, a move affecting an estimated 378,000 students.</p> <p>During public comment at a district Generative AI Ad Hoc Committee meeting after Google’s announcement, Hillary Power, a parent and member of Schools Beyond Screens, called for Los Angeles Unified to continue “formalizing, extending and expanding the current T-K to 12 moratorium of student use of generative AI.”</p> <p>“Google has been particularly clear on their priorities when several days ago they issued a monopolistic and, frankly, pathetic ultimatum to schools to use their AI search products or be cut off,” she continued.</p> <p>The district has asked Google for a way to turn off the AI summaries, according to LAist, and said it is reviewing Google’s announcement and its “potential implications for schools, educators and students.”</p> <p>Miami-Dade County Public Schools, the nation’s third-largest district, may be headed in a different direction.</p> <p>In 2023, the district blocked AI chatbots such as ChatGPT over concerns about cheating and misinformation, according to The New York Times. Two years later, it reversed course, offering Gemini for Education to about 100,000 high school students, the largest AI deployment in a U.S. school district at the time. In February, the district announced a continued collaboration with Google to give all its high school students free access to full-length SAT practice exams through Gemini.</p> <p>But on Sept. 16, the Florida Board of Education voted to require public school districts and charter schools to set rules for AI use. Schools will also have to tell parents and guardians which AI tools students will interact with, so families can opt their child into a tool or request a non-AI alternative. Districts have until July 1, 2027, to comply.</p> <p>Miami-Dade County Public Schools did not respond to questions about how it will adapt to Google’s policy and the new Florida rules.</p> <p>Former Federal Trade Commission chair Lina Khan criticized Google’s decision to tie Search to its AI summaries. In a Nov. 3 post on X, she said the company is using its search monopoly to “coerce schools into adopting AI.”</p> <p>“Multiple courts have found Google liable for violating antitrust laws, and this latest move shows it remains unchastened,” she said. “As the public increasingly pushes back against the use of AI in schools, Google is making it clear it will punish those who don’t fall in line.”</p> <p>Khan isn’t the only one questioning whether the move is legal. Zephyr Teachout, a professor at Fordham Law School, said she does not find Google’s claim that it cannot unlink AI Overviews from Search “credible,” and that the company could be found to violate antitrust laws governing how companies tie the sale of two products.</p> <p>In antitrust law, “tying” is when a company with significant market power requires customers who want one of its products to take another as well. Google’s parent, Alphabet, is No. 5 on the 2026 Fortune 500. According to the FTC, tying raises antitrust questions when it “restricts competition without providing benefits to consumers.”</p> <p>“Google ran search for decades without AI, has filters that allow people to see results without AI summaries, and has been inconsistent in how it has responded to the tying claim — merely asserting they are ‘deeply integrated’ is not a legal argument,” Teachout said. “The company has on/off controls for K-12 administrators and could have an on/off control for search and AI.”</p> <p>Google has lost a tying case before. In April 2025, U.S. District Judge Leonie Brinkema found that Google had been “willfully acquiring and maintaining monopoly power” in two parts of the online advertising market and that it had illegally tied them together by pushing websites that wanted full access to its ad exchange to also use its ad server.</p> <p>But the company has also won a case involving AI Overviews. In September, U.S. District Judge Amit Mehta dismissed a lawsuit by Penske Media over Google’s use of its content for AI Overviews, finding that Penske had not plausibly shown that Search and AI Overviews were separate products.</p> <p>Teachout said schools are different, pointing to the “clear evidence of a separate market demand in schools for search as distinct from AI.”</p> <p>Whether a court ever takes up such a case, or agrees with Teachout, is an open question. Google’s changes take effect early next year, and until then, districts have to decide whether keeping Google Search is worth accepting the AI that comes with it.</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://fortune.com/2026/11/21/google-search-ai-overviews-schools-chicago-nyc-los-angeles-miami/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; text-decoration: none; font-weight: bold;"> → Baca artikel asli </a> </p> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5283"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "Frosinone, Raimondo: \"Alvini mi dice: 'Dembele corre dietro a tutti, perché non puoi farlo tu?'\"", "image": ["https://tmw-storage-auto.tcccdn.com/storage/tuttomercatoweb.com/img_notizie/thumb3/51/51e1b58f415c0c50356263cd963c5f7a-29722-oooz0000.jpeg"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T11:38:51", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T11:38:51", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5283"}</script> <h2>Frosinone, Raimondo: "Alvini mi dice: 'Dembele corre dietro a tutti, perché non puoi farlo tu?'"</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.tuttomercatoweb.com/serie-a/frosinone-raimondo-alvini-dice-dembele-corre-tutti-puoi-farlo-tu-2295896" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://tmw-storage-auto.tcccdn.com/storage/tuttomercatoweb.com/img_notizie/thumb3/51/51e1b58f415c0c50356263cd963c5f7a-29722-oooz0000.jpeg" alt="Frosinone, Raimondo: "Alvini mi dice: 'Dembele corre dietro a tutti, perché non puoi farlo tu?'"" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; color: #333;"> <p>Antonio Raimondo, attaccante del Frosinone, ha voluto ringraziare nella sua intervista a SportWeek il tecnico Massimiliano Alvini e il direttore sportivo sportivo Renzo Castagnini: "Hanno creduto in me fin da subito, nonostante venissi da una stagione negativa, e questo mi ha dato ancora più forza e consapevolezza nei miei mezzi. Prima, magari, boh (fa una smorfia)... Invece con lui da subito ho capito che è fondamentale. Ci mostra dei video del Paris Saint-Germain, mi guarda e fa: 'Se Dembele, che ha vinto il Pallone d'oro, corre dietro a tutti, perché tu non puoi farlo?'. E io rispondo: 'Giusto, certo che posso farlo'. Mi ha migliorato nel lavoro spalle alla porta, in cui non ero molto bravo, e nell'attacco alla profondità, che comunque era già il mio pezzo forte, insieme ai movimenti in area. Non per niente, tutti i miei gol sono arrivati dentro l'area di rigore".</p> <p>Tra i tanti talenti del Frosinone ci sono anche Ghedjemis, Kvernadze e Schmid: "I primi due hanno nel dribbling e nel tiro a giro sul secondo palo il pezzo forte. Il secondo è anche potente e veloce nel dribbling. Schmid ha visione di gioco e assist, anche se a me non ne ha servito ancora uno".</p> <p style="margin-top: 25px;"> <a href="https://www.tuttomercatoweb.com/serie-a/frosinone-raimondo-alvini-dice-dembele-corre-tutti-puoi-farlo-tu-2295896" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener" style="color: #0d6efd; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> </div> </article> <article class="article-card" id="artikel-5334"> <script type="application/ld+json">{"@context": "https://schema.org", "@type": "NewsArticle", "headline": "How to turn off GTA 6 auto uploads on Xbox", "image": ["https://cdn.mos.cms.futurecdn.net/N6rB5F3mBGz4tn4FbYbZkF-2560-80.jpg"], "datePublished": "2026-11-21T11:37:37Z", "dateModified": "2026-11-21T11:37:37Z", "mainEntityOfPage": "https://railfansday.github.io/berita1jam/technology.html#artikel-5334"}</script> <h2>How to turn off GTA 6 auto uploads on Xbox</h2> <div class="article-meta"> 📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: <strong>Technology</strong>  |  <a href="https://www.gamesradar.com/games/grand-theft-auto/gta-6-xbox-auto-uploads/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow">Read Source →</a> </div> <div class="article-body"> <img src="https://cdn.mos.cms.futurecdn.net/N6rB5F3mBGz4tn4FbYbZkF-2560-80.jpg" alt="How to turn off GTA 6 auto uploads on Xbox" loading="lazy"> <div style="text-align:center; margin-bottom:20px;"><img src="https://cdn.mos.cms.futurecdn.net/N6rB5F3mBGz4tn4FbYbZkF-2560-80.jpg" alt="How to turn off GTA 6 auto uploads on Xbox" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy"></div> <div style="text-align:center; margin:20px 0;"> <script type="text/javascript"> atOptions = {'key' : 'f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2','format' : 'iframe','height' : 90,'width' : 728,'params' : {}}; </script> <script type="text/javascript" src="https://upholdbrilliancerecast.com/f9e40eb3b3cd05f1038aac7b36b33ad2/invoke.js"></script> </div> <div class="article-body" style="font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.8; color: #333;"> <p>Join the GamesRadar community for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.</p> <p>Keep exploring and earning more as a member.</p> <p>Get the biggest gaming news, reviews, and releases straight to your inbox.</p> <p>Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more</p> <p>Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.</p> <p>Your weekly update on everything you could ever want to know about the games you already love, games we know you're going to love in the near future, and tales from the communities that surround them.</p> <p>Our special GTA 6 newsletter, with breaking news, insider info, and rumor analysis from the award-winning GTA 6 O'clock experts.</p> <p>From the creators of Edge: A weekly videogame industry newsletter with analysis from expert writers, guidance from professionals, and insight into what's on the horizon.</p> <p>Hardware nerds unite, sign up to our free tech newsletter for a weekly digest of the hottest new tech, the latest gadgets on the test bench, and much more.</p> <p>Sign up to our new Switch 2 newsletter, where we bring you the latest talking points on Nintendo's new console each week, bring you up to date on the news, and recommend what games to play.</p> <p>Get sneak previews, exclusive competitions and details of special events each month!</p> <p>Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.</p> <p>You could risk a ban by auto-uploading GTA 6 clips to the Xbox Network</p> <p>When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.</p> <p>If you're playing GTA 6 on Xbox, you should turn off your console's automatic upload feature or you could risk having your Xbox account suspended. An Xbox user on Reddit reported receiving a two-day, unrepealable ban for automatically uploading a 28-second clip of GTA 6 characters at the beach. According to the Reddit user, sangobar, the clip was almost immediately flagged by the Xbox Network for either violating the Xbox Community Standards or Microsoft Services Agreement terms (the email they received from Xbox doesn't specify which). However, the user claims the clip only showed characters in beachwear and the clip was therefore incorrectly flagged.So, to avoid the risk of an Xbox ban it is better to be safe than sorry, to disable your Xbox automatic upload feature, and to understand what Xbox's guidelines are around the content you can upload to the Xbox Network.</p> <p>Automatic uploads to the Xbox Network are enabled as standard on Xbox, meaning the GTA 6 clips you capture on your console are automatically uploaded to the Xbox Network. To turn off your Xbox Series X's automatic uploads feature, follow these steps:</p> <p>One Drive is more lenient in what you can upload, but if you'd rather not automatically upload any of your GTA 6 capture, it's best to set both it and Xbox Network to 'Don't Upload'.</p> <p>GTA 6 clips could potentially violate the 'Keep your content clean' term of the Xbox Community Standards, specifically the 'Make stuff that's suitable for all audiences rule', which states: "The Xbox community must be friendly and accessible to everyone, this isn't the appropriate place for sexual content. Keep your public posts and messages suitable for all ages and audiences."</p> <p>According to Xbox, some examples of content that could violate this term include sharing or distributing non-consensual intimate imagery, links or imagery that displays pornography, screenshots or game clips that include mature sexual content, custom gamerpics that contains nudity, lewd messages to other players, and distasteful or inappropriate pictures. In other words, be careful before sharing images or clips of GTA 6's more mature content, like GTA 6 strip clubs.</p> <p>© GamesRadar+.
